The fourth move of the multiplayer plan. **The shape is the modules' own:** `map.serializeMap` (every loaded chunk whole: the floors run-length, the walls with their state, the props by their anchor with their live fields, the items on the floor, the pending wild spawns; a chunk not loaded at the save regrows from the file and the seed), `serializeCharacter` (everything plain on a character as it is; the target and a carried prop by id; an auto-walk, a timed action, a breach, a bubble dropped; a body's grid holds the same entries the body wears, made one again by uid on the way back), `game.serialize` (the clock, the stepped seconds, the generator's four words, the world's time scale and id counter, the sleepers by chunk key, every character), as **a deep copy JSON can carry** (nothing shared with the live world; `Infinity` and `NaN`, which JSON turns to null, go as tokens: a plain wall's hp, a zombie's time since it saw anyone). `game.restore` loads the map file afresh for its authored source, installs the saved chunks over it (the authored props the load made for those tiles superseded), makes every character by its own creator and gives it its saved fields, links by id, dresses, and puts the counters, the clock, and the generator back last (the creators roll). **The slot** (`src/save.js`): one, in the browser, versioned, discarded whole on a mismatch; the title offers **Continue, Day N, HH:MM** when it holds one (and Play reads New game); the pause menu's **Save game** is live; the world **autosaves every 60 s of game time** and when the tab is hidden or left, only after Play or Continue and never while dead. **Death writes the save with the survivor dead**, and the title then refuses Continue ("Your last survivor died") until the next survivor exists (`31/05` item 34; the 2026-10-08 ruling that the world outlives the character stands, and the world is kept in the slot for it): a reload after a death cannot take the last minute back. **Zombie ids come from the world's counter** (`nextId("z")`, saved with it) instead of a module counter that a restore would have restarted into saved names; the fresh-boot hash moved once with that (`308b389f` without the dev chest, `1cef42ac` with it, which the pane has on: the chest's id moves every id after it, so `node tools/world.js --devchest` is the pane's number). **Verified:** `node tools/world.js valley 600 --roundtrip` (630 KB of save at step 300, a fresh world restored from it in 70 ms, the hash after the other half the same); `check("save")` six of six (a messy world serialised, put back, and serialised again is the same text; a world stepped on from its save hashes as the one never saved); on 8011 the title's Continue brought back hp 42 at the tile left, the clock, 87 chunks and 55 dead, and the world ran on; Save game said "Saved."; a death wrote the dead save and the title refused it; growth, sleep, sway, water, eviction, craft, wear, the headless checks. Not saved, by the parent's rule: an auto-walk, a timed action's progress, a draw, arrows in flight, the burning's particles, the interface. Deferred: a save as a file to carry to a friend's machine (`31/03`), the next survivor (item 34).

### 2026-10-13 — The world boots with no window: `node tools/world.js` hashes what the pane hashes (tier 0 of `31`, item 23 of `31/05`)

The third move of the multiplayer plan. `game.create({ json, look, devChest, combatTesting })` is the world stage's simulation part (the generator seeded, the map loaded and the spawn's ring grown, the dev chest, the player with the kit, the authored dead and villagers, the ring and its wild dead, the rooms, the testing dead), the same calls in the same order in the browser's boot and under Node; `game.hashText()` is the world as the determinism check's text, which the check now reads from the world. **Headless (`assets.setHeadless`, `assets.setJsonReader`):** JSON from a reader the runner sets (the disk) instead of `fetch`; a sheet is its frames, anchor, and animations with no picture (`figure.describeFigure` for the figures: what a bite's active frame and a corpse's finished fall read), no PNG is loaded, no canvas is made. `loop.init(hooks)` sets the hooks without a frame, for a world driven by `advance`. Three things that touched the DOM at the wrong time are guarded: `boot.js`'s module-scope lookup of the loading screen, `showMessage` with no screen (the line still goes to the log, which has none either), `nextFrame` with no document. **The proof:** `node tools/world.js` boots the valley from the same files in 96 ms, steps it 600 times in 55 ms (0.09 ms a step, no renderer), and prints `48ba67d2` over 62 entities: the pane's number. The pane after the move: determinism `48ba67d2`, growth 9 of 9, sleep 6 of 6, sway 8 of 8, water 8 of 8, eviction 5 of 5, craft 9 of 9, wear 4 of 4. What this is: the authority of `31/04` as a thing that runs; what it is not yet: a save, a server, or a second player.

### 2026-10-13 — The world's state and the step order leave `main.js` for `game.js` (tier 0 of `31`, item 17 of `31/05`; `23a` D1's `game.js`)

The second move of the multiplayer plan, on its branch. `src/game.js` holds what is the simulation's: the state (`info`, `player` and `players`, `zombies`, `corpses`, `villagers`, `others`, `progress`), `setPlayer`, `nearPlayer` (near any player: the thinking box becomes a union), and the step in two parts: `begin(dt)` (the rules, the clock, the fires, the chunks grown round each player and dropped behind) and `step(dt, snapshot)` (the player from the mouse and the keys as a snapshot, the arrows, the burning, the timed action, the dead and the villagers near anyone, the fallen and the turned, death as a `gameOver` event the client turns into the dead screen, the rooms refound, the light). `main.js` is the client: boot, the view and the camera, the keys, the panels, sight, the ground, the renderer, and it calls `begin` before its frame work and `step` after, so the order of the one-file step is kept. **No reference to a window, a canvas, the keyboard, or the DOM in `game.js`**; the world's small fields (the time scale, the id counter) stay in `world.js`, which the leaf modules import, and `game.js` is imported by `main.js` alone, so there is no cycle. **Verified:** the valley's fresh-boot hash `48ba67d2` unchanged; the frame hash identical to the build before the split at the middle and the wide zoom (`400eb7a3`, `62672bdd`; `282e5b25`, `5c64cf1c`; a first comparison differed only because two origins keep two zoom settings); growth 9 of 9, sleep 6 of 6, sway 8 of 8, water 8 of 8, eviction 5 of 5, light 5 of 5, sheets 172, the headless checks. One rule found by the frame hash and kept explicit: `light.update` reads current rooms, which the client's `roomAt` used to refind a line before; the world now calls `map.roomsIfDirty()` itself before its light.

### 2026-10-13 — Step 3 of `30` built: the stations, recipes, durability, the tools; the rulings the build forced

**Any lit fire that cooks is a cooking station** (the hearth, the campfire, the inn's hearths): the spec's pot on the hearth is not asked for until the pot is an item worth placing; a row then adds it to the fire's definition. **Ingredients come from everything within reach**: the hands, the belt, your bag, the station's own output, and every container within 2.6 tiles that has been searched (a container's bag exists once it is opened; an unsearched chest holds nothing yet, which is the loot rule, not a crafting one). **The station keeps working while you walk off and while you sleep**: its queue runs in the world's time, so a bed beside the oven bakes the bread by morning; the fire's going out pauses it, never spoils it. **A prop recipe lands beside the station** on the nearest free tile, never in your hands; a chest costs four planks, a workbench five and a log, so the second workbench is yours to carry home. **A worn-out weapon leaves the hand with a word**, nothing dropped, as `30` says; **the finisher's extra damage means a stick may see a sixth off**, which the check allows (the number is a design number, the wear is by hits). **Tools are weapons of five kills** and swing badly; their verbs come with the steps that need them (till with the farms, mine with the quarry), so this step only gives them bodies, recipes, and the anvil's hammer rule (the knife, the hatchet, the pickaxe, the scythe, the spear, the mace, the sword ask for a hammer in hand; the hammer itself and the iron bar do not, so the loop can start). **The habit forks, taken the habit's way:** the recipe queue lives on the prop with the maker's id, not in the panel; Make calls `verbs.run("craft")` by the station's id and nothing else; a Use row names its verb by `action` (the label stays `verb`), so a second player's click resolves through the same table. **Open:** the loom and the dye vats wait on cloth and wool as items (step 7 or 8); the recipe numbers are first guesses, Michael's to tune in `data/recipes.json`.

### 2026-10-13 — Step 2 of `30` built: the needs, under `31/08`'s habits; the rulings the build forced

**The multiplayer plan's six habits are followed from this step on** (`docs/31-multiplayer/08`, another session's branch until it lands; Michael made this session the authoritative build agent): sleep is a field on the world the loop reads (`world.timeScale`), not a dt multiplier or a clock jump; the sleep verb is a row in a new verb table addressed by the bed's id; every prop has an id and every item entry a uid from the world's counter; a fire's feed and burn-out are one mutator each that touch the tile and emit. **Where the habit forked against the simpler way, the habit won:** the sleep verb could have been a closure on the bed's action and the fast-forward a multiplier in the loop; both are the other way, so a command queue can call the same door later. **The sleeper stands by the bed:** the rig has no lying pose; one comes with the art pass. **Waking:** rested and morning (06:00 to 22:00), whichever is later; a bed taken away wakes you. **The forge burns charcoal**, an item added now (the burners of step 6 supply it; until then the dev chest). **Survival is on by default** (hunger, thirst, tiredness drain; the setting still turns it off). **The rates are to the day:** hunger a day and a half, thirst a day, tiredness a day and a quarter, as `30` set; to feel out in play.

### 2026-10-13 — Reading never grows a chunk: the world is the simulation's alone (tier 0 of `31`, item 8 of `31/05`)

The first move of the multiplayer plan (`31-multiplayer/`), made on its own branch and small enough to merge under the big pass. Until tonight `map.tileAt` grew a missing chunk on any read, and the renderer, the ground bake, the light, sight, the arrow's scan, and the placement previews all read: so which chunks existed, in what order the wild's dead were made, and where the one generator stood depended on the window's size and the zoom, and a world run with no window (a server) would have diverged from a browser's at its first wild chunk. **Ruled by the build, Michael to overrule:** `tileAt` is for reading and never grows; beyond the grown ring it returns **the blank tile** (plain floor, no walls, no prop, outdoors, frozen so a write to it throws). Growth is **`map.ensureAround`'s ring round each player, four chunks** (was three in play, four at boot; the ground now needs a viewed chunk's neighbours grown, below), and **the mutators'** (`growTile`: `dropItem`, `placeProp`, `setFloor`, `buildWall`, `destroyWall`, `addBracket`). **The ground bakes a chunk only once it and its eight neighbours are grown** (a bake reads `REACH_MAX` tiles into them), draws nothing for one that is not, and never caches a picture of blank land; in play the ring is wider than any view by a chunk, so nothing in view is ever without its picture. Boot grows the spawn's ring before anything reads. **Verified:** `check("growth")` nine of nine (a far read is the blank and grows nothing; the light, sight, a frame at every zoom, and a bake of an ungrown chunk grow nothing; a mutator grows exactly its chunk); the valley's fresh-boot hash `48ba67d2` the same over four boots in two tabs at the middle and the wide zoom, one tab with a zero-size window (before, a boot at another zoom grew other chunks); the ground check as on `main` (its `Deterministic` row grows the far chunk and its neighbours first, as a visit would; the `Lit as before` and `Budget` rows fail on the valley on `main` too, they look for the square's campfire); rooms, light, eviction, water, the headless checks unchanged. The law in `CLAUDE.md`.

### 2026-10-12 — The big pass answered (Michael): the needs, the stations, the OSRS rule, durability by kills, the bucket, animals last, sleep as a fast-forward

The fifteen questions of `22`, re-asked against `29`, and the thoughts that followed, now law and written up as **`30-the-big-pass-spec.md`**, which supersedes `22` as the plan. **Needs:** hunger, thirst, and **sleep** in 1.0 (a tiredness moodle; sleep only in a bed, indoors), no cold until seasons, never boredom or sanity. **Health** regenerates slowly while fed and watered, faster asleep, not at all hungry or thirsty; bandages for bleeds; nothing else; when the infection is made real, a craftable cure with the mushrooms joins the cauldron. **Crops need water**, forgivingly: a dry plot pauses, never dies. **No spoilage.** **Stations:** `E` opens the panel; ingredients from the bag and every container within reach; a craft takes time and you may walk away while it works; the forge, the oven, and the cooking hearth need their fire, and **a fire is maintenance** (fed now and then, never a hassle). **Recipes all known**, one file. **"Nothing interesting happens":** any item on any item, any tool on anything, with that default; a real interaction replaces the message with its confirmation or count (OSRS's lesson, Michael's call). **Materials:** wood, stone, iron, charcoal, cloth from wool and flax, herbs, mushrooms; leather with the animals. **Durability is kills, and low:** a stick or plank about five dead, the best weapon no more than a hundred; the game will not have six hundred dead to kill, so the smith's supply is the balancer; tuned up if unfair. **Tools:** hatchet, pickaxe, hoe, scythe, shovel, knife, hammer, all poor weapons; **the bucket is a prop carried on the head** like a bed, filled at any water, poured on a plot, never in the bag. **The wheelbarrow** one big immovable grid. **Two animals, last:** chickens (eggs to check for, "definitely cosy") and deer (the bow's quarry, the knife's skinning, leather, the tannery back), as the pass's final step so nothing before depends on them. **The dev chest and combat testing** as a setting on the real map. **The home** is the end cottage on the south lane. **Magic** stays `24`'s. **Sleep fast-forwards the clock and the simulation**, restricted to a bed indoors, never interrupted ("I do not care if local zombies walk around during that time"), and you get up; the co-op tiebreaker applied: the sim runs fast, nothing is poked. **Drink and fill from every large water** as from the well. **Nothing waits for art:** a bookcase is a box with a definition until drawn.

### 2026-10-12 — Walking through a field makes it wobble (Michael)

"Some but not all of those interactable objects can be pathed through, crops are the big one": any character, the player, the dead, or a villager, walking through an interactable that can be walked entirely through sets it swaying as a click would, which is what makes a field read as a field: the movement interacts with it. Trees and rocks are excluded by the same test, `solid` on the prop (`interact.brushThrough`, called for everyone each step). One round finishes before the next starts (the sway's own 560 ms is the gap), so the sway is never restarted mid-way; walking on keeps it going, standing still in it does not. A definition's `"sway": false` or `clickAnim` holds here too.

### 2026-10-12 — The wobble written in stone, and the bug that had switched it off; the container its own panel; capitals in the headers (Michael)

**The rule, in stone:** anything interactable wobbles when clicked, by default; a sprite definition turns it off with `"sway": false`, and one with a `clickAnim` plays that instead (the well's bucket). **The bug:** nothing had wobbled since step 2 of `23` moved the poke's stamp to the sim clock (`interact.pokeAt`, sim milliseconds) while the renderer still subtracted it from the wall clock (`render/props.js`, `swayOf` and `clickFrame`), so every sway was over before it began; the renderer now reads the sim clock, the one the stamp is in. The frame check's frozen wall clock did not catch it because no check pokes anything; a check for the sway is owed. **Layering:** the bag, the character screen, the belt, and the build list sit above the speech bubbles and the bars over the dead (`z-index` 7 over 6), the menu above them (9), the screens above all (10). **Art in the bag** keeps the whole cell: the cutout edge is painted as inset shadows, not a border, and a stack of art stays centred (the throwing knife and axe sat low and clipped). **An open container is its own panel**, dragged and remembered apart from the bag (`panelPos.container`); its home is to the left of the bag; its X closes it alone. **Panel titles are capitals** (`.inv-title`), to see whether that feels overbearing: the pixel font's lowercase vowels were hard to read at the header's size.

### 2026-10-12 — The body stays the rig's; the design agent draws what goes on it (Michael), and three fixes to the rig

Michael tried to have the design agent redraw the base body and found the rig's body "actually nearly perfect": the head's shape kept, the arms right in a way the agent could not reproduce (one capsule hanging from a shoulder joint inside the torso, the near arm in front and the far arm behind by the depth sort, smooth in every facing because it is projected, never drawn). **Ruled:** the body is the rig's numbers and rules (`src/figure/rig.js`, `draw.js`); the design agent is asked for what goes on it (garments, hair, hats, beards, held things), painted as parts on the template, never for the body. **Three changes to the rig, Michael's, 2026-10-12:** (1) **the hip two pixels higher** (`BODY.hip` 15 to 17): the body a smidge smaller, the legs a smidge longer; (2) **a far arm shows a real sliver of skin or nothing** (`FAR_ARM_MIN` 8 fill pixels in `draw.js`; `Frame.cullPart`): the one or two pixels that peeked beside the hip in the side and three-quarter facings, outline against outline, are gone; (3) **no double outline**: in any row where an arm's outline runs straight against another part's outline with no fill between, the nearer part's outline stays and the other pixel becomes what lies beyond it, fill or nothing (`Frame.mergeOutlines`; Michael: "if there isn't a column of skin between two borders, don't draw both"). To make the rules possible the outline pixels now carry the part that drew them; a painted layer still never overwrites an outline. The feet stay as they are (Michael is split on drawn feet; a `feet` layer can change them later). **A console fix on the way:** `__game.setLook({})` now forces a rebake, so an edit to `figure.BODY` shows at once (no key ever included the rig's numbers, so it never did before). **Michael's second look, the same night, three more and then "good enough to stop touching for a good long while":** a far arm's shoulder half is judged apart from its hand's and shows only when most of it does (`FAR_ARM_MIN_UPPER` 24 against the hand's 8), so no strip or shoulder flare along the torso in the three-quarter views while the wrist and hand still peek out at the hip; the torso's front-to-back thickness is the arm's (`torsoD` 3, was 4), so from the side the neck is as wide as the arm, not a block wider than it; the hips are a hair thicker than the legs (`hipD` 3.4, was 3), so the legs' tops never jut a pixel past the torso in the side views. **His play, the same night, found two regressions of the far-arm rule and fixed its shape for good:** a holding arm lost its upper arm (the strip along the torso was under the count), and walking toward or away the back-swung arm lost its hand (a short whole arm has few pixels too). **The rule is now about width, not count:** what is left of a hidden arm goes only if no row of it is wider than two pixels (`FAR_ARM_SLIVER`), judged per half so a hand stays when the shoulder is a sliver; and **a raised arm is never judged** (a pitch over `RAISED_ARM` 40 degrees: holding, swinging, the dead's reach). A frame-by-frame bake of the walk and of the hold at every facing is how it was checked.

### 2026-10-12 — Step 2 of `23` done; what it deferred, and the two checks that prove a move

The engine is hardened as `23a` listed (robustness, determinism, growth, ownership), every move a commit with the profile before and after, and the sim's fresh-boot hash the same from the step's first commit to its last. **Deferred by the spec's own defaults, not dropped:** the `actions/` and `map/` splits (D2, D4: not in step 3's way), the pathfinder's allocation (B8: never the biggest spike), and renaming what `main.js` now is (boot's stages, the state, the step order, `render()`) to `game.js`. **A ruling the moves forced:** a split of a drawing module is accepted only when `__game.check("sheets")` (every sheet's pixels) and `check("frame")` (a rendered frame, the wall clock frozen) hash the same before and after; the renderer's animations run on the wall clock by design, which is why the frame check freezes it. Nothing in the sim reads the wall clock; the renderer may.

### 2026-10-12 — Far wild chunks are dropped and their dead sleep with them; the fallen leave the thinking list (step 2 of `23`, group B)

**Ruled by the build, Michael to overrule:** a wild chunk that play has not changed (no floor set, no prop placed or felled, no item dropped or taken, no wall) and that lies more than **eight chunks** from the player is dropped from memory, unless a villager stands in it; the wild is deterministic by hash, so it grows back the same chunk, and **the characters that stood in it sleep on its record and wake when it grows back**, so nothing is lost and nothing is rolled twice (verified: living plus sleeping 116 before and after a 400-tile round trip). This is **a first slice of `03`'s records**, not the milestone: no hopping, no alarm, no lure, no trickle. **A corpse leaves the dead's list for a corpses' list**: drawn, looted, and burning, never iterated by the AI, separation, speech, or the bars. **The light cache is invalidated per chunk** (a change touches its chunk and the eight around it, the reach of any source under a chunk), and **rooms are refound locally** on a wall built or taken down (the two tiles the edge separates name the rooms to refill; outdoors is one region a refill may join). The per-step cost of the village did not change with these; what changed is that it no longer grows with the distance walked.

### 2026-10-12 — The simulation gets one generator and one clock (step 2 of `23`, group C; the co-op law applied)

Michael asked that the engine plans be checked against the multiplayer tiebreaker (`CLAUDE.md`; his interest is general, not pressing). The check is written into `23`, step 2, and this is the first fork it decided: **the simulation rolls only through `src/rng.js`**, one seeded generator advanced in step order (the map file's `seed`, default 1), rather than a per-call list of exempt `Math.random`s; **the sim reads time only from `loop.simTime()`** (game seconds stepped), and the bow's "a draw held through a pause is dropped" rule counts the loop's pauses instead of a wall-clock gap; **the rules the sim reads from the settings are a snapshot** (`src/rules.js`), refreshed once a step, so a host could hand a guest the same object and a test in the pane no longer changes the sim by poking storage. Exempt by design: `fire.js`'s embers and smoke (a visual), the wild's `hash2` (position-keyed is better than sequence-keyed there), the renderer's animation clocks. **Verified:** two boots, 600 steps each, the same hash over 71 entities (`__game.check("determinism")`). The checks live on from here: `node tools/check.js` headless, `tools/checks/` in the pane.

Built and measured the same night (`23`, step 1, "As built"). **Rulings the numbers forced:** the dev server speaks **HTTP/1.1 with keep-alive** (two hundred files took 18 s over one connection per file; 250 ms after); **the atlas is packed tallest first** at boot (registration order filled it to 94 percent, sorted 67), and **the figure sheets are the atlas's budget**: eighteen figure-sized sheets are the bulk of it, so a second atlas page is scheduled for the day the wardrobes grow (`25`) rather than now; the ground kit's sheets and the `zombie` definition's generated body never enter the atlas (never drawn); **boot never waits on an animation frame alone** (a hidden tab gets none; a 40 ms timer stands in). **Kept as specced:** the loading screen is not skippable and shows its stages; everything in the manifest loads and every outfit bakes before the title; the manifest's stamps cache art for a year while source and the manifest stay `no-store`; saves are refused from another origin.

### 2026-10-12 — The third-hit rule off for testing, and the finisher cut to 1.5 (Michael)

To test the melee without them: **the third-hit knockdown is switched off** (`combo.enabled: false` in `data/combat.json`; the code and its hit counting are kept, `hits` and `window` still there) and **the downed-enemy bonus is 1.5, not 2** (`finisher.mult`). **Effect, measured:** the sword's two-swing kill (34 + 68) is gone, as the mace's: with a 1.5 finisher a first-hit knockdown gives 34 + 51 for the sword and 38 + 57 for the mace, both short of 100, so a fresh 100-hp dead one now takes 3 swings with either, every time (79 of 80 trials; one outlier from the test's own repositioning). The mace keeps its 60 percent knockdown chance, so a mace still floors them more often, it just does not shorten the kill. The knockdown remains the "crit without being one" for what it does next (the dead one on the floor for 2 s, hit at 1.5x), but it no longer shortens the kill. Both are one line each to put back.

### 2026-10-12 — Dual wielding on the left button, with an exposure price (Michael, question 23)

The right button is the menu, so an off-hand swing there never worked; **ruled: dual wielding is on the left button.** Any one-handed weapon (sword, hatchet, mace, stick, torch) goes in either hand; clicking faster than the main weapon's recovery swings the off hand, so spamming goes back and forth at up to twice the rate with two strong weapons. **The drawback, Michael's: glass cannon.** The first idea was an anti-shield (more damage taken in front, more at the sides, fatal from behind). **Built as state, not loadout (Claude's refinement):** an off-hand swing exposes you for 2.5 s, and exposed you take 1.5x from the front, 2.5x at the sides, a hundred times (fatal) from behind; carrying a torch or two weapons costs nothing, because a loadout penalty would tax the ordinary night kit (a sword and a torch) and the torch only swings at a click rate the sword cannot keep. Open: whether the fatal back should be a big multiple that kills in two bites instead (`behind` in `data/combat.json`). Answers question 23 (option b, with a price).

### 2026-10-12 — Stagger by weight, immunity, and a bite under way is not cancelled (Michael: "the big one", question 29b)

**What it was:** every hit staggered the dead one for 0.3 s and cancelled a bite it had begun, and the push on every hit sent it back a step, so a player who kept hitting held a zombie in a stun-lock and it never got near enough to bite. **What it is now:** (1) **a hit staggers by chance, by weapon weight** (`staggerChance` on the weapon; `stagger.chance` 0.5 is the default): stick and plank 0.3, torch and fists 0.2, hatchet, sword, spear stab 0.5, halberd sweep 0.7, **mace, halberd thrust, spear throw, and the shove always (1)**. (2) **After a stagger ends, the dead one cannot be staggered again for 0.6 s** (`stagger.immunity`), so a stream of hits cannot hold it. (3) **A bite under way is not cancelled by a stagger** unless the weapon `interrupts` (the mace, the thrust, the throw, the shove); a knockdown still cancels anything. Damage, the push, and the knockdown chance are unchanged (the third-hit rule is still off; Michael's earlier cuts stand). **Measured** (a scripted player spamming a sword at a charging crowd, aggro on, 5 trials): five zombies from all sides landed **7.8 bites a fight against 6.0 under the old rules** (30 percent more), and a lone zombie against a sword still lands none (it dies on the way in), while a stick lets one bite through half the time; the player in that test died in both, with bleed and infection now live. Not changed, for Michael to judge by play: the push per hit (0.27 tiles for the sword) still sends a zombie back a step; and the spin-click question (a turn-rate cap) is the other half of 29b and is not built.

### 2026-10-12 — Weapon durability: permanent, hits only, evaporates (Michael); and a big pass to come

Michael, thinking about the dual-wield balance: **durability is wanted, and as a one-way thing: a bar that only goes down, wears only on a landed hit (never on a miss: a fairness rule), and when empty the item evaporates; no repair, no mending, no gradual dulling.** It waits for a crafting and smith loop to feed it (the weapons are consumables, so the smith and crafting stations are their supply). Claude's earlier "repair at a station" idea is withdrawn. **Large inventories and the bookkeeping they bring are wanted** ("it's how I would want to play"); Claude is to say when performance becomes a concern (the sprite atlas is the first thing to watch). A crowd breaking weapons is not a worry yet and would be a sign to ease something. He has a **big "what should we add" pass planned**: smith, cooking and crafting stations, a wheelbarrow (an immovable crate), seeds for each crop, many tool models, new interfaces, and a rigorous way to handle item interactions: drafted as a to-do list with questions in `22-the-big-pass.md`.

### 2026-10-12 — Bleed stacks, with Michael's two bounds (question 29b)

**Built (`src/bleed.js`), before the stagger fix, so it can be tested together with the infection marker.** A landed bite that did damage opens a wound with a **50 percent** chance (`bleed.chance`; measured 190 of 400): one more stack, up to **5**, the timer back to **12 seconds**. The wound drains health by stack count, **0.4, 1.6, 3.6, 6.4, 10 a second** for 1 to 5 stacks (`dpsByStacks`: a table that rises faster than the count, because **Michael's bounds** are two: **five fresh stacks kept refreshed, with no other damage, must take 8 to 10 seconds or more to kill from full health** (he counted it out in Mississippis: measured **10.0 s**; four stacks 15.6 s, three 27.8 s), **and a single stack, unless you are already very low, is a nuisance** (**under 5 health in all**: 4.8, and it closes by itself)). Unrefreshed, three stacks cost about 43 and four about 77 before they close, so the wound is the thing to bandage. **A bandage** (new item, stacks to 5, "Apply" in its menu, in the testing chest and `giveKit()`) closes it at once. A moodle ("Bleeding" to "Bleeding out", a drop for now) shows the stack count; a message names the first wound. **Not done:** bleed from anything but bites, a bandage from cloth, health potions (Michael: later, needs balancing), the sound and the drips.

### 2026-10-12 — The virus comes back as a marker (Michael, question 29b)

Michael, on the wounds question: the **stack-bleed** is liked, health potions are an idea for later and need balancing around, and **the virus is wanted back because it adds stakes**: infection "alarming but not uncommon", a thing to take care of that **should not turn you inside one to two in-game days**, with ways to mitigate it that feel fair (an idea: mushrooms, some crafting). **First step, built as a mock-up (`src/infection.js`):** a landed bite that did damage (after the guard and any exposure) infects with a **20 percent chance** (`infection.chance` in `data/combat.json`); the infection is a clock of **1.5 in-game days** (`infection.days`, 1080 real seconds at 720 per day) that only shows itself, as a **moodle going green (Bitten), yellow (Fevered), orange (Sick), red (Turning)** at a quarter each, held red after. **It does nothing else: no turning, no death, no damage.** A message says the bite broke the skin. Measured: 66 of 400 landed bites infected (chance 0.2); the stages and the tooltip texts follow the clock; `__game.infection.infect() / cure() / progress()` for testing. The stagger and no-cancel fix is next (question 29b, Michael: "the big one"), then the bleed.

### 2026-10-12 — Multi-hit is the easy button, off by default (Michael)

After a long play with sword and shield (no trees in the testing mode, so easier than the real map): **holding right to guard and backing away with a horde trailing has recreated Zomboid's walk-backwards play, and Michael likes it a lot**; he never uses multi-hit in Zomboid because it is the easy button, and it feels overpowered here for the same reason, "which tells you we've built a coherent combat system". **Ruled: the Multi-hit setting stays in the menu, 100 percent, and is off by default as the intended way to play** (the default was on; his own pane already had it off). No special rule for the guard: it already cuts what a blow does to 35 percent. **Open for him:** with multi-hit off by default, the halberd's sweep loses its crowd identity and becomes a forgiving-aim first-target blow against the thrust's narrow reach; one answer is to make the halberd's sweep cleave regardless of the setting (the only weapon that does, paid for in its slow swing and 12 stamina); not done.

**The halberd tuned** the same night after "potentially OP": a slower swing and a larger stamina cost on both modes, a slightly shorter reach, a longer drawing (`21`).

### 2026-10-12 — The halberd's thrust and a longer picture (Michael)

Michael loved the second mode and asked for a longer drawing to match the green hit outline. **Built:** a thrust on the middle button or `Q` (reach 3.0, a 12 degree arc, 40 damage, 1.1 s; tuned the same night to reach 2.9, 1.3 s, 15 stamina, `21`; nearest only), and a held picture that can be larger than 32 px with its own grip (the halberd's is 112 px, 52 px of pole forward of the hand). **Any weapon can carry an `alt` block now.** `21` has the numbers.

### 2026-10-12 — The weapon order, and the halberd (Michael)

**Design principle: the mace is the strongest (crush the skull) and the sword second (cut off the head), even if they end up overtuned.** The halberd, roughed out the same day: two-handed, reach 2.3, damage 30 (below the sword), 0.8 s swing, no shield; a second mode (thrust on the middle button) is for later. Numbers and a measured kill time in `21`.

### 2026-10-12 — The knockdown is the game's crit (Michael's finding, `14`)

Michael: a sword knockdown on the first swing makes the follow-up a two-shot, "it feels like a crit without actually being one." **Measured (120 kills of a fresh 100-hp dead one per weapon, real swings, 2026-10-12):** sword 2 swings 32 percent of the time and 3 otherwise (mean 2.68, 1.34 s); mace 2 swings 67 percent (mean 2.33, 1.52 s); hatchet 3 or 4 (mean 3.5, 1.93 s); spear stab 3 or 4 (3.55, 2.13 s); stick 5 or 6 (5.5, 2.75 s). **The rule behind it:** every hit has a knockdown chance (0.3; the mace 0.6); a hit on a downed one is a finisher at twice the damage; so a first-hit knockdown kills in two when `damage + 2 x damage >= 100`, which is `damage >= 34`: the sword (34) and the mace (38) have it, the hatchet (30), spear (30) and stick do not. **It is emergent, not designed:** the sword clears the line by two hit points (34 + 68 = 102 against a 100-hp dead one; at 33 it vanishes), and the dead's hit points moving, a tired swing (half damage), or a guarded one (35 percent) all break it. **Ruled: keep it, and call the knockdown the crit**: the chance is visible (the dead one hits the floor) rather than a hidden roll on damage, which is why it feels fair. What to decide only if the sword proves too strong overall: the sword's knockdown chance (0.3), its damage by one point, or its stamina; and if the dead gain varied hit points, whether the two-shot line should be a stated rule (a `heavy` weapon tier) instead of an accident of 34.

### 2026-10-12 — The guard: total in front, half at the sides, attack from behind it (Michael, `21`)

After "Fixed!!": attacks are allowed from behind the guard at much less damage (35 percent); walking guarded and the free draw both slow to 0.45; **a zombie's bite is one event with fixed damage, so the guard works as a scale of it by the attacker's position against the aim: nothing in front (100 degrees), half to the sides (220 in all), whole from behind**, which Michael hoped was possible and it is; blocks cost 5 stamina and **no stamina regenerates while the guard is up**, so it cannot be lived in (Claude's addition, to keep the fight grindy rather than a stalemate). Numbers in `21`; the first build's "nothing while guarded" rule is struck.

### 2026-10-12 — The hands remember what a two-hander pushed out (a bug of Michael's finding, `21`)

Flipping through weapons with a shield on lost the guard for good: the bow pushed the shield into the bag and nothing put it back. **Ruling (Claude's, small):** an item pushed out of the off hand by a two-handed one is remembered and returns, once, when a one-handed item takes the main hand with the off hand empty, or the two-hander is put away, if it is still in the bag or on the belt. Michael may want it narrower (the shield only) or off; the memory is `player.offMemory` in `inventory.js`.

### 2026-10-12 — The guard shows; the bow's free draw slowed (Michael, `21`)

Options 1 and 2 for the guard's legibility are built (the raised shield, the spark, the flash, the stamina pulse; the raised-guard pose is for the art). **The bow's free draw goes from 0.9 s to 1.4 s, the braced draw stays 0.6 s**, so the two modes are distinct and the bow loses the race to melee (about 4.9 s a kill free, 2.5 braced, the sword 1.5): the safe weapon is the slow one. Michael: "the shooting mechanics are perfect", so only the numbers move; `21` lists the knobs in the order to turn them, including the dead learning where a shot came from.

### 2026-10-12 — Combat difficulty and infection are one dial (Michael, after the first play of sword and spear)

Michael fought a crowd with the new weapons: eight kills, a spear thrown, a panic, a death: "working as intended". The principle he asked to be written down: **with no infection mechanic, combat has to be hard enough on its own to justify the dead being a threat at all** (a bite that costs nothing lasting must still cost something in the moment: positioning, stamina, a thrown spear that leaves the hand empty). **If an infection mechanic is added later (`05`, "Infection, treatable"), combat must be eased to match**, or the two together read as punishment and not as tension. Either lever may move; they must be set together. A crowd that one player on a good run can just barely clear is the target feel for now. **Michael's softening, the same day, on adding the infection marker:** "maybe easing combat to match": ease it only if real consequences do not land somewhere that feels balanced; he does not want to force himself to keep tweaking, and the caveat is that if infection's real effects feel right alongside the combat as it is, nothing is eased.

### 2026-10-12 — The mouse in combat, built (`21`)

All four steps of `21` are built on Michael's rulings below: the middle button and `Q` for the main hand's other mode (`Q` because `R` rotates a carried or dragged thing), the right button's tap (the menu, on release) and hold (the off hand), the spear's stab on the left and throw on the middle with a 0.5 break chance and the `noDual` rule, the bow's brace and right-tap cancel, a shield and its guard, and the off-hand swing. **Claude's picks, all tunable and in `21`:** a tap is 0.2 s of simulation time; **the guard and the brace rise on the press, not after the tap time** (a defence that arrives late is no defence), so only the menu waits; the off-hand swing does wait for the hold, or every swing would also open the menu; **nothing is swung, stabbed, drawn, or thrown while the guard is up** (Valheim's rule, to keep spear and shield from being the best of everything); the stab's 1.9 reach, 40 degree arc, damage 30; a torch counts as a light, not a weapon, for `noDual`; no raised-guard or thrust pose yet.

### 2026-10-12 — The mouse in combat: three roles, the Zomboid split, and the special boy (Michael, question 29)

**Left is the main hand's primary; right is the off hand (hold to block with a shield, the off-hand weapon or torch when dual wielding); middle click, with a key twin, is the main hand's other mode** (the spear's throw; later weapons with two modes, a halberd). Accepted fully, with one caveat kept open: Michael may yet put both blows of two one-handed weapons on the left button if left-and-right feels clunky; Claude's version is built first and play-tested. **Spear and shield is intentional and special**: the only hand pairing with no empty role (stab, block, throw), so it gets drawbacks: the spear may break on a throw (about half the time, to tune), and spears are big in the grid, so "one shield and fifty spears" is not a thing; the throw is a last resort with consequences. A spear can be held with a shield, **never with another one-handed weapon**. Spears throw from the middle button and bows loose from the left, and that is fine: they are different weapons that only share an aiming system the player never sees (no guide by default). **The actions menu stays on the right button, Zomboid's way: a tap opens it, a hold blocks.** Kept because the menu is a debug view of everything a thing offers, and because the right button is the only honest home for click-to-move, the left being "swing the held thing" and "poke the interactable". **A held block slows the player by about a fifth to a third and makes them much safer**, in a form to design with the shield. **The bow brace** (Michael, the same hour): hold right with a bow to move slower and draw faster, two draw speeds to master, never one in the middle; a tap of the right while drawn cancels the draw without loosing (he had meant to ask for it; it felt natural). Numbers to start from: braced movement 0.2, draw 0.9 to 0.6 seconds. None of this is built yet: the spear's stab, the throw moving to the middle button, the block, the brace, and the split are the next combat work after the fire test below.

### 2026-10-12 — Characters can be set on fire, a visual test (Michael)

Every character's menu, the player's own figure included, offers **Set on fire**, then **Put out**: flames, embers, and smoke on the figure, an orange pulse on its sprite, and the burning body as a moving light at night (`fire.js`; `05-someday.md`, "Burning", has the look-up). No effect on anything, and the dead do not notice the flame. Not the ground, not props, not until "someone is paying me to play this game".

Claude's picks, the tunable ones: speed 14, an arc peaking between 6 and 70 px, a draw time of 0.55 s (faster than the bow's 0.9), a 0.45-tile push, a throw pose that arches the arm back. `lodged` became a map by item id for it.

Built as decided (hits at landing only, the arrow leaves at the bow's height, the cursor read at chest height); `20` has the numbers. ### 2026-10-12 — The bow's draw (step 2 of `20`)

Built: with a bow in the main hand the left button is the draw (it no longer swings or pokes), the arrow is spent at release, and the aim is the cursor read at chest height. Rulings the build forced: a pose style that sets its own off arm (the bow's string hand) wins over the both-hands holding pose; a draw dropped by sprinting, an open panel, unequipping, or a pause spends nothing; a release inside 15 percent of a draw is a dry one; 0.22 s of recovery after a shot; a cursor closer than a tile still shoots a tile out. The pose uses the attack frames held by hand, so the legs do not stride while drawn (art and polish, later).

### 2026-10-12 — The corpse is a container, drawn as what is left on it (step 4 of `19`)

A dead zombie is searched like a chest (`Search body` on E and in the menu, `Open body` after), and its grid is the body: taking a garment from it takes it off the body and the corpse is redrawn the same step, down to the undies; putting it back dresses it again. A body is one baked frame (the last of its fall, in the facing it died in), so redrawing it costs nothing the atlas notices. The pockets roll from the existing `body` loot table; nothing that does not fit the grid is lost. Claude's picks: the grid is 6 × 6; the E prompt reaches a body within the usual 1.15 tiles; a body cannot be searched until its fall is over; corpse sheets are never evicted yet (no body is ever removed).

### 2026-10-12 — A tiebreaker for single-player forks: prefer what a second player could use (Michael)

Co-op was looked at for feasibility (`05-someday.md`): possible, large, and the sync trouble Zomboid has is avoidable with one authority. It is not scheduled, and single player is finished first. **The rule, in `CLAUDE.md`:** when a single-player implementation forks and one path makes multiplayer easier while the other bakes in the single perspective, take the multiplayer-friendly path, all else equal. It only breaks ties; it never justifies building multiplayer machinery early.

### 2026-10-11 — One poke for everything you can touch: swing and sway (Michael)

Michael wants the "touch to get" economy to start as one default response, an in-world to-do list of what needs work later. **Rulings:**
- **Trees are clickable too.** They were always the same kind of game object as the rocks and mushrooms (props on tiles); they are now marked `interactable` like them. "Chop down" stays on the right-click menu, and right-clicking anywhere on a tree, its crown included, offers it.
- **A left click swings as usual** (the attack, whatever it hits), and the rock, mushroom, or tree under the cursor sways. Right-click sways it and opens its actions, if any.
- **The sway replaces the jitter:** the base stays put and the top leans one way, then the other, dying away over 0.56 s (a skewed quad, `skew` in `gl.quad`); tall things lean further, from a pixel and a half for a mushroom to five for a giant tree.
- **Authoring:** rocks, mushrooms, and trees can be placed on a building's lawn the same way the trial rocks were, as `props` in the map file; the floor stamp, when it exists, gets them as palette entries.

### 2026-10-11 — Sparser again, and big rocks in the wild (Michael, art direction)

An art-direction pass, not a performance one. Measured over the same test area, before and after: trees 983 to 750 (density a quarter lower: the scatter 0.018 to 0.117, the wild 0.0405 to 0.1305), mushrooms 270 to 135 (forage chance 0.021), leaf decals 407 to 224 (`perReach` 0.3, `extraChance` 0.22, `openChance` 0.05). **Big rocks join the wild's generator** (`wild.rocks` in `square.json`): each 40-by-40-tile patch has a 60% chance of one rock at a spot rolled from the patch, in place of whatever tree stood there; at Michael's monitor size (about 2,000 tiles a screen) 144 sampled screens showed 0, 1, or 2 rocks (74, 58, 12) and never more. The village keeps its three placed rocks. Performance gains are small but real: fewer sprites to sort and draw each frame, and slightly cheaper bakes (7.5 ms median, from about 8.7).

### 2026-10-11 — Mushrooms and rocks become game objects that wiggle (Michael)

Michael's redrawn art (bigger mushrooms outlined all the way round; rocks with a curved, bedded base and top faces seen from above) and a ruling: mushrooms and rocks are clickable game objects now, with no stats or actions; a click makes one wiggle on a short cooldown, "I recognize you clicked me". **Built:**
- **Mushrooms are props, not decals.** A `forage` rule in `square.json` places them near trees (a tree within two tiles, 0.042 per tile, five kinds), inside the bounds at load and in the wild as chunks grow, from pure rolls (`treeAt` answers where trees are without making chunks, so the same mushrooms come back whatever order chunks load in). Each sits a few pixels off its tile's centre (a prop `offset`) so they do not line up. Walkable; not carriable.
- **Rocks** take the new art; anchors sit midway between the base's lowest point and its ends.
- **The wiggle:** a click on an interactable's visible pixels (`interactable: true` on its sprite definition) shakes it side to side, about two pixels, dying away over 0.42 s; it will not wiggle again for 0.6 s. Right-click pokes it (and opens no "Walk here" menu); **left-click pokes it instead of swinging**, since clicking on a thing means the thing. The test is per pixel, so a click beside a mushroom still swings.

### 2026-10-11 — The ground, step 3 built (Michael accepted step 2; rulings the build made)

Step 3, the flat decals, is in (status in `15-ground.md`). Rulings the build made: decals lie on grass only, except litter, twigs, and pebbles, which may lie on dirt too, set per rule by an `on` list in `data/ground.json`; decals are drawn before the shadows, so a tree's shadow falls across its litter; a chunk draws decals from tiles two beyond its edge, which leaves room for decals about twice today's size; a litter count is `floor(reach - d)` plus the extra roll; ties for the nearest tree go to the first found in scan order; every decal is mirrored or not by its own roll. **Open for Michael:** with reach 2 to 4, almost no grass is "open" (1 to 8%), so flowers and open-ground litter nearly never appear; one data change fixes it if he wants them.

### 2026-10-11 — The ground, step 2 built (Michael accepted step 1; rulings the build made)

Asked whether the dirt gets texture: yes, step 4 generates a seamless world-space dirt texture, replaceable by drawn art. Step 2 is in (status in `15-ground.md`). Rulings the build made: canopy darkening uses straight-line distance from the trunk with a fade over the last tile of reach, not the brief's Manhattan count, so it is an even ellipse on screen with no visible rim; the shadow's horizontal radius is `shadow.width` × the frame's width, which is the mockup's ellipse (the spec halved it by mistake, now corrected); the tint falls on grass only, never on laid floors; shadows fall on whatever ground is under them, dirt included.

### 2026-10-11 — The ground, step 1 built (rulings the build made)

Step 1 of `15-ground.md` is in and verified (status there). Rulings the build forced: the bake is a single per-pixel copy from each owner tile's source, no `drawImage`, because which tile owns a pixel and where it falls in the tile's frame are the same for every chunk and are precomputed once; grass lies under every tile including laid floors, and an antialiased edge pixel of a generated floor frame is taken at full alpha, so a pixel is always one thing; the bake uploads its ImageData directly; dirt and farm lose the generated tile outline (`outline: false` on the flat placeholder), which was the grid on the road; until step 4 the earth kinds are hard diamonds. The spec's screen offset sign was a typo and is corrected.

### 2026-10-11 — The ground is scoped: milestone 9 (Michael, scope by Claude)

Michael's four points (kill the grid and texture and blend the ground; the kit's decals as optional layers whose art may grow; it must work in the infinite forest; write the cobble rule now because cobblestone roads are coming) are answered in `15-ground.md`, scoped for implementation by another model. **Rulings made by the scope, all overridable in `data/ground.json` or the map file:** the ground is baked per chunk on the CPU in Canvas 2D and drawn as one quad through the existing shader (the brief's GPU dirt pass is the same maths run once at bake time; one code path, and the picture can be saved and looked at); the checker is gone everywhere and a faint grid shows only in build and remove mode; the kit's darker grass green is the grass now; **cobblestone is allowed** (re-deciding the vision's "no cobbles"), muted and warm, never concrete grey, and **always inside a dirt verge, never touching grass**, with the village square cobbled by default and the roads left dirt; the earth (grass, dirt, farm, cobble) blends with ragged pixel-crisp edges and laid floors (wood, stone) stay hard diamonds; trees cast a baked stepped shadow sized by the sprite; all decals bake flat for now; canopy reach and litter counts are per tree (2, 3, 4 tiles for the three tiers); felling a tree takes its shadow and its litter with it; the kit's missing textures (dirt, cobble, edge noise) are generated at boot behind the usual sprite definitions. The brief's flip-shares-a-bit-with-the-variant flaw is fixed with an independent roll.
